**Mains hook:** Use the memory anchors to show agency and limit.
**Back:** *"Gotami founds the nuns, Visakha gives: anchors of memory, limits of power."*

### Flashcard ANC-2155 — Śramaṇa movements and social change: gifts, critique and admission limits
**Front:** How did renunciant movements relate to the social order of the Buddha age?
**Definition (learn):** Renunciant communities operated within the urban-rural, political and household world. Lay gifts from rulers, gahapatis, seṭṭhis, women and others supported monastic communities. Ethical criticism of sacrifice, birth pride and attachment challenged parts of the dominant hierarchy. Admission could cross some birth boundaries, but rules could exclude or regulate debtors, slaves, soldiers and others. Buddhist texts criticise the moral sufficiency of birth but do not constitute a modern programme of universal anti-caste equality. (✅ owner 13 §16)
**Breakdown:**
- Gifts sustain monasteries (✅ owner 13 §16)
- Critique of sacrifice, birth pride, attachment (✅ owner 13 §16)
- Admission limits: debtors, slaves, soldiers (✅ owner 13 §16)
- Economic change explains audiences and institutions, not the full content of doctrine (✅ owner 13 §16)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2025, ANC-2039, ANC-2144, ANC-2151
**Trap:** "Jainism and Buddhism were anti-caste merchant revolts" is wrong (✅ owner 13 §19).
**Mains hook:** "Economic change explains who joined and how institutions grew; it does not explain karma, non-self or ahiṃsā." (✅ owner 13 §16)
**Back:** *"Gifts fund, critique questions birth, rules still bar debtors: reform, not revolution."*

### Flashcard ANC-2156 — The Buddha and kings: political ethics, cakravartin and dhamma as norms
**Front:** Which rulers and clans does Buddhist tradition place the Buddha in dialogue with, and what status do cakravartin and dhamma have?
**Definition (learn):** Buddhist traditions place the Buddha in dialogue with Bimbisāra and Ajātaśatru (Magadha); Prasenajit/Pasenadi (Kosala); Licchhavis/Vajjians and other clan polities; Śākyas and kin-based elites; householders, merchants, artisans and labourers. Political ethics: restraint, just conduct, welfare, non-arbitrary punishment, generosity. Wheel-turning king (cakravartin) and dhamma are normative models, not an administrative constitution. Aśoka's dhamma must not be back-projected into the Buddha's lifetime. Patronage stories do not prove exclusive royal conversion. (✅ owner 13 §17)
**Breakdown:**
- Rulers and clans in dialogue (✅ owner 13 §17)
- Ethics: restraint, welfare, punishment (✅ owner 13 §17)
- cakravartin/dhamma: normative (✅ owner 13 §17)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ashoka later (✅ owner 13 §17).
**Links:** ANC-2044, ANC-2055, owner 14 (Aśoka's dhamma)
**Trap:** "Kings were converted" and "Ashoka's dhamma existed in the Buddha's lifetime" are both unsafe. (✅ owner 13 §17)
**Mains hook:** Use the normative model to explain ethical rulership without overclaiming.
**Back:** *"Cakravartin and dhamma are models, not constitutions; Ashoka comes later."*

### Flashcard ANC-2157 — Later Vedic versus Buddha-age society: a trend comparison
**Front:** How does Buddha-age society differ from the Later Vedic world on polity, economy, society and religion?
**Definition (learn):** Polity: Later Vedic kin-based chiefdoms and emerging territoriality; Buddha-age larger monarchies and clan oligarchies with stronger territorial extraction. Economy: Later Vedic agrarian intensification, cattle and ritual redistribution; Buddha-age town-route-craft-coin networks with agrarian growth. Society: Later Vedic sharpening varna prescription; Buddha-age varna norms plus visible gahapati, seṭṭhi, occupational and dependent groups. Religion: Later Vedic expanded sacrifice and internal Upaniṣadic critique; Buddha-age diverse renunciant institutions and lay-monastic networks. (✅ owner 13 §18.1)
**Breakdown:** Four axes (✅ owner 13 §18.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Trend, not binary (✅ owner 13 §18.1).
**Links:** ANC-2021, ANC-2131, ANC-2158
**Trap:** Do not present a binary or pan-Indian replacement. (✅ owner 13 §18.1)
**Mains hook:** "Compared with the earlier Vedic world, Buddha-age society was more urban, more monetised and more politically differentiated." Qualify each.
**Back:** *"Later Vedic chiefs and sacrifice; Buddha-age towns, coins, gahapatis and monks: a trend, not a break."*

### Flashcard ANC-2158 — Ganga-basin limit: Buddha-age society is not a census of India
**Front:** What is the regional scope of the Buddha-age evidence, and what must not be generalised?
**Definition (learn):** The clearest evidence concerns the middle Ganga basin, eastern Uttar Pradesh, Bihar and connected north Indian routes; it cannot be generalised unchanged to the north-west, Deccan, far south or forest zones. North-western, central, peninsular and forest societies followed different material and political sequences. (✅ owner 13 §10, §18.3)
**Breakdown:**
- Clear zone: middle Ganga basin, eastern UP, Bihar, routes (✅ owner 13 §10)
- Not generalisable: north-west, Deccan, far south, forests (✅ owner 13 §10)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2134, ANC-2157, ANC-2019
**Trap:** "Buddha-age society" is not an all-India census. (✅ owner 13 §18.3)
**Mains hook:** Use the regional limit as a counterpoint in any national-scope answer.
**Back:** *"Ganga basin is the lens, not the whole subcontinent."*

### Flashcard ANC-2159 — Advanced thesis: "Cities, Kings, and Renunciants" and the Magadhan imperial matrix
**Front:** What is the advanced owner's core thesis, and how does it link to the Mauryan matrix?
**Definition (learn):** Upinder Singh frames the period as "Cities, Kings, and Renunciants": urban growth, state formation and new religious movements must be studied together. The political map contained monarchical states (rājyas), oligarchic states (gaṇas/saṅghas) and tribal principalities; the 16 great states differed in size, constitution, economy and military capacity. The 4th–3rd centuries BCE Magadhan imperial direction, Nandas and Mauryas, emerges from this matrix. (✅ owner 13 advanced §1, mini-timeline)
**Breakdown:**
- Three political forms: rājya, gaṇa/saṅgha, tribal principality (✅ owner 13 advanced §1)
- The 16 states differed in size and capacity (✅ owner 13 advanced §1)
- Magadhan imperial matrix (4th–3rd c. BCE) (✅ owner 13 advanced mini-timeline)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6th c. BCE 16 great states (✅ owner 13 advanced mini-timeline).
**Links:** ANC-2063, ANC-2131, ANC-2139
**Trap:** The "republic" word is the trap; ancient gaṇa-saṅghas were not modern republics. (✅ owner 13 advanced §5)
**Mains hook:** "Cities, kings and renunciants" as a one-line GS-I thesis (✅ owner 13 advanced §7).
**Back:** *"Cities, kings, renunciants: the one-line thesis for the Buddha age."*

### Flashcard ANC-2160 — Trap register: Buddha-age state and society
**Front:** Which unsafe claims about Buddha-age state and society must be corrected?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's trap register includes: the age began suddenly in 600 BCE; Pāli, Jain and Brahmanical texts describe society neutrally; kings were absolute and republics democratic; iron caused cities, states and surplus; NBPW or one coin proves urbanisation; coinage was universal and state-minted; varna was the fully formed later caste system; Vaiśya equals every merchant and Śūdra every labourer; land was fully private or wholly royal; women had no agency or Buddhist equality; Buddhism and Jainism were anti-caste merchant revolts; the Mauryan bureaucracy already existed. (✅ owner 13 §19)
**Breakdown:** Twelve unsafe claims with corrections (✅ owner 13 §19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed.
**Links:** ANC-2131, ANC-2136, ANC-2137, ANC-2143, ANC-2149, ANC-2150, ANC-2152, ANC-2155
**Trap:** Telltale words: "suddenly", "neutrally", "absolute", "universal", "fully", "alone".
**Mains hook:** Use the corrections to qualify any Buddha-age answer.
**Back:** *"Suddenly, neutrally, absolute, universal, fully, alone: six words to challenge."*

### Flashcard ANC-2161 — Answer architecture: Buddha-age society (10, 15, 20 marks)
**Front:** What answer skeletons does the owner give for Buddha-age state and society questions?
**Definition (learn):** 10 marks/150 words: define the problem; three named dimensions; norm-versus-practice or regional limit; two-line judgement. 15 marks/250 words: source base → state institution → agrarian/urban mechanism → social groups → varna norm versus lived complexity → counterpoint → verdict. 20 marks/300 words: chronology/region → sources → agrarian and urban base → state/extraction → households/occupations → varna/jāti/status → gender → śramaṇa response → limits → synthesis. Paragraph: claim → named evidence → inference → limitation. (✅ owner 13 §20)
**Breakdown:** Three skeletons and paragraph formula (✅ owner 13 §20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Word limits as stated.
**Links:** ANC-2131, ANC-2160, ANC-2157
**Trap:** Do not mix Vedic society, Buddha-age society and Mauryan empire in one statement. (✅ owner 13 basic §5 trap)
**Mains hook:** "Analyse how surplus extraction and standing armies transformed janapadas into mahājanapadas." (✅ owner 13 §7)
**Back:** *"Norm, practice, region, limit: four checks before the verdict."*

### Flashcard ANC-2162 — PYQ coverage note: zero direct PYQs for Topic 13
**Front:** Which PYQs are routed to Buddha-age state and society, and which adjacent items are used?
**Definition (learn):** Repository ledgers contain zero direct Topic-13 Prelims or Mains PYQs through 2026. Adjacent practice: a 2026 printed question on Pāli coin evidence (not routed to Topic 13); 2025 region-river mapping (Topic 11); 2024 Buddha epithets (Topic 10); 2024 Later Vedic comparison (Topic 09); 2023 geographical factors (Topic 03). These must not be reported as direct Topic-13 routing. (✅ owner 13 §21)
**Breakdown:**
- Direct: none (✅ owner 13 §21)
- Adjacent items listed (✅ owner 13 §21)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ledgers checked across 2018–2023, 2024–2025 and 2026 (✅ owner 13 §21).
**Links:** ANC-2135, ANC-2095, ANC-2054, ANC-2019, ANC-2028
**Trap:** Do not report adjacent items as direct routing. (✅ owner 13 §21)
**Mains hook:** Use the adjacent Later Vedic comparison (ANC-2019) to practise social-economy change.
**Back:** *"No direct Buddha-age PYQ: five adjacent items borrow the knowledge."*

### GROUP — The Mauryan Empire (basic owner 14 + advanced owner 14)

### Flashcard ANC-2163 — Concept: Power and Piety — the layered Mauryan empire
**Front:** What is the core verdict on the Mauryan Empire, and how do power and piety fit together?
**Definition (learn):** The Mauryas were the first very large subcontinental empire, with Pāṭaliputra as capital and a multi-tiered structure of core, provinces, corridors and mediated frontiers. Upinder Singh frames the age as "power and piety": empire-building and Ashoka's ethical project must be read together, not as power versus piety. Control varied by region. (✅ owner 14 basic §1; ✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Core: Magadhan base and Pāṭaliputra (✅ owner 14 §12)
- Provinces under royal princes or officials (✅ owner 14 basic §1)
- Mediated frontiers and differentiated control (✅ owner 14 §20)
- Ashoka's dhamma as imperial ideology with Buddhist roots (✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 324–187 BCE (Upinder Singh); conventional c. 324/321–187/185 BCE (basic §4).
**Links:** ANC-2164, ANC-2177, ANC-2203, ANC-2208
**Trap:** Do not reduce the Mauryas to Arthashastra machinery or to Ashokan non-violence alone (✅ owner 14 basic §5).
**Mains hook:** "Assess whether the Mauryan Empire was a highly centralised state or a layered empire with varied control." Verdict: first large empire, strong institutions, uneven reach, exceptional inscriptional legacy.
**Back:** *"Power and piety together: a layered empire, not a modern bureaucracy."*

### Flashcard ANC-2164 — Mauryan chronology ladder with confidence labels
**Front:** What is the Mauryan chronology, and which dates are secure?
**Definition (learn):** Chandragupta c. 324/321–c. 297 BCE; Seleucid settlement c. 305–303/302 BCE; Bindusara c. 297–c. 273/268 BCE; Ashoka accession/consecration c. 273/268 (varies); Kalinga war (8th regnal year) conventionally c. 261 BCE; Ashoka's death c. 232 BCE; end of dynasty c. 187/185 BCE. R.S. Sharma uses 273–232 BCE for Ashoka. (✅ owner 14 basic mini-timeline and §4; ✅ owner 14 §9.2)
**Breakdown:**
- Regnal years in inscriptions are stronger evidence than one absolute date (✅ owner 14 §9.2)
- Kalinga figures are Ashoka's royal statement, not audited totals (✅ owner 14 §9.2)
- Puranic tradition: Bṛhadratha the last Maurya, killed by Puṣyamitra (✅ owner 14 §9.2, §21)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kalinga 261 BC; Ashoka 273–232 BCE (R.S. Sharma); c. 187 BC end (basic mini-timeline).
**Links:** ANC-2094, ANC-2173, ANC-2174, ANC-2204
**Trap:** "Ashoka founded the Mauryan Empire" is wrong; Chandragupta did (✅ owner 14 basic §5). "Kalinga happened before Ashoka's reign" is wrong; it was in his reign (✅ owner 14 basic §5).
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to avoid absolute dates in a 20-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Chandragupta 324/321, Seleucus 305, Bindusara 297, Kalinga 261, Ashoka dies 232, end 187/185."*

### Flashcard ANC-2165 — Megasthenes' Indica: lost original, fragmentary quotations
**Front:** What is Megasthenes' Indica, and what must be said about its reliability?
**Definition (learn):** Megasthenes was the Greek ambassador sent by Seleucus to Chandragupta's court; his Indica describes Pāṭaliputra, city administration, the court and society. The original is lost; later Greek and Roman quotations select, paraphrase and contradict. (✅ owner 14 basic §1, §2; ✅ owner 14 §9.1)
**Breakdown:**
- Ambassador for Seleucus (✅ owner 14 basic §1)
- Pāṭaliputra and court (✅ owner 14 basic §2)
- Six committees of five for Pāṭaliputra (later quotations only) (✅ owner 14 §13.5)
- Seven social groups; no-slavery claim conflicts with other evidence (✅ owner 14 §15.1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Late Greek quotations; original lost (✅ owner 14 §9.1).
**Links:** ANC-2170, ANC-2171, ANC-2185, ANC-2190
**Trap:** "Megasthenes is a neutral eyewitness whose fragments all agree" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §23). "Megasthenes proves there was no slavery" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §23).
**Mains hook:** Use Megasthenes as an external court view, with fragments as a limit.
**Back:** *"Seleucus sent him; the book is lost; the quotations choose."*

### Flashcard ANC-2166 — Arthashastra: layered prescriptive political economy, not a Mauryan manual
**Front:** What is the Arthashastra, and how should it be used for Mauryan administration?
**Definition (learn):** The Arthashastra is a sophisticated prescriptive model of kingship, revenue, espionage, law and political economy; a composite and layered text with a debated date. It is not Chandragupta's verbatim constitution and is not the only source for Mauryan administration. It discusses crown land (sīta), produce share (bhāga), taxes, tolls, fines, labour obligations, superintendents, mines, forests, salt, trade and workshops. (✅ owner 14 basic §1, §5; ✅ owner 14 §9.1, §14.1, §14.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Model, not verbatim manual (✅ owner 14 §9.1)
- Samāhartṛ and sannidhātṛ as prescriptive categories (✅ owner 14 §13.1)
- Control model for mines and trade, not universal monopoly (✅ owner 14 §14.4)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Date debated (✅ owner 14 §9.1).
**Links:** ANC-2181, ANC-2183, ANC-2189, ANC-2165
**Trap:** "Arthashastra is the only source" and "Arthashastra describes Chandragupta's state directly" are both wrong (✅ owner 14 basic §5; advanced §5).
**Mains hook:** Use Arthashastra as theory and test it against inscriptions and archaeology.
**Back:** *"Kautilya theorises; inscriptions show; Megasthenes watches from outside."*

### Flashcard ANC-2167 — Ashokan inscriptions: the richest contemporary royal voice
**Front:** What do Ashokan inscriptions establish, and what do they not establish?
**Definition (learn):** Ashokan inscriptions are contemporary royal statements that establish royal titles, edict chronology, dhamma, Kalinga, officers, communication, audiences and some places. They are not a neutral social survey or complete administrative archive; they focus on royal ethical communication rather than routine administration. (✅ owner 14 §9.1, §20; ✅ owner 14 advanced §2)
**Breakdown:**
- Establish: titles, dhamma, Kalinga, officers, communication (✅ owner 14 §9.1)
- Do not establish: neutral social survey; full administration (✅ owner 14 §9.1)
- Edicts show intention more clearly than enforcement (✅ owner 14 §8)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Major and Minor Rock Edicts; Pillar Edicts (✅ owner 14 advanced §4).
**Links:** ANC-2173, ANC-2175, ANC-2196, ANC-2197
**Trap:** "Ashokan edicts are mainly administrative circulars" is wrong; they mainly convey dhamma and political ideas (✅ owner 14 advanced §5).
**Mains hook:** "Compare sources: inscriptions reveal royal ideology; Arthashastra theorises statecraft; Megasthenes gives an external court view." (✅ owner 14 advanced §7)
**Back:** *"Ashoka speaks to his people: the edict is intention, not audit."*

### Flashcard ANC-2168 — Evidence rule: attestation, tradition, model, correlation and inference
**Front:** Which five evidence categories must be separated in any Mauryan answer?
**Definition (learn):** Separate contemporary attestation (Ashokan edicts), later literary tradition (Mudrārākṣasa and Buddhist/Jain narratives), prescriptive model (Arthashastra), material correlation (archaeology, coins) and scholarly inference. (✅ owner 14 §9.1 evidence rule)
**Breakdown:**
- Contemporary attestation: edicts (✅ owner 14 §9.1)
- Later literary tradition: Mudrārākṣasa; Sri Lankan chronicles (✅ owner 14 §9.1)
- Prescriptive model: Arthashastra (✅ owner 14 §9.1)
- Material correlation: pillars, Kumrahar, coin hoards (✅ owner 14 §9.1)
- Inference: scholarly (✅ owner 14 §9.1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Source classes matrix (✅ owner 14 §9.1).
**Links:** ANC-2166, ANC-2167, ANC-2169, ANC-2201
**Trap:** Do not treat one source class as proof of another. (✅ owner 14 §9.1)
**Mains hook:** Use the five labels to write any evidence-led paragraph.
**Back:** *"Attest, tradition, model, correlate, infer: five labels before each claim."*

### Flashcard ANC-2169 — Chandragupta: Nanda overthrow, Sandrokottos and later memory
**Front:** How is Chandragupta's rise recorded, and what is contemporary and what is later?
**Definition (learn):** Greek writers identify Chandragupta as Sandrokottos/Androkottos, placed in the post-Alexander world. Buddhist, Jain, Brahmanical and dramatic traditions disagree on his birth and path to power. Chāṇakya/Kauṭilya and the Nanda overthrow are central to later memory; the Mudrārākṣasa plot is not contemporary evidence. Nanda fiscal-military concentration and north-western instability supplied opportunities; success still required conquest, alliance, organisation and legitimacy. (✅ owner 14 §10.1)
**Breakdown:**
- Sandrokottos/Androkottos (Greek) (✅ owner 14 §10.1)
- Traditions disagree on birth (✅ owner 14 §10.1)
- Nanda overthrow; Chāṇakya (later memory) (✅ owner 14 §10.1)
- Justin/Pliny army figures rhetorical (✅ owner 14 §10.1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Accession c. 324/321 BCE (⚠️ reconstructed) (✅ owner 14 §9.2).
**Links:** ANC-2084, ANC-2094, ANC-2123, ANC-2168
**Trap:** Chāṇakya is not a verified contemporary of the early Mauryan court; treat the drama as later literature (✅ owner 14 §10.1).
**Mains hook:** Use Chandragupta's rise to show opportunity plus organisation.
**Back:** *"Sandrokottos in Greek, Chanakya in drama, Nandas fallen: opportunity plus organisation."*

### Flashcard ANC-2170 — Seleucus and Chandragupta: the c. 305 BCE settlement
**Front:** What does the classical tradition say about the Seleucus–Chandragupta settlement, and what is disputed?
**Definition (learn):** Seleucus I campaigned east around 305 BCE; classical tradition records a settlement in which Seleucus relinquished or recognised Mauryan authority over territories east of his frontier (Arachosia, Gedrosia, Paropamisadae debated). Chandragupta supplied 500 elephants (a literary figure). "Epigamia" may indicate a marriage alliance; a named dynastic marriage is not certain. (✅ owner 14 basic mini-timeline; ✅ owner 14 §10.2)
**Breakdown:**
- c. 305–303/302 BCE (✅ owner 14 §9.2)
- Territorial clauses debated (✅ owner 14 §10.2)
- 500 elephants (literary figure) (✅ owner 14 §10.2)
- Epigamia: marriage alliance interpretation debated (✅ owner 14 §10.2)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 305 BC (basic mini-timeline).
**Links:** ANC-2117, ANC-2164, ANC-2171
**Trap:** Do not state a named dynastic marriage as certain. Do not say the settlement erased local powers. (✅ owner 14 §10.2)
**Mains hook:** Use the settlement to show north-west integration without uniform governance.
**Back:** *"Seleucus gives up the east, Chandragupta gives 500 elephants: a literary number, a diplomatic fact."*

### Flashcard ANC-2171 — Megasthenes as Seleucid ambassador
**Front:** What is Megasthenes' role in the Seleucid–Mauryan diplomacy?
**Definition (learn):** Megasthenes later served as Seleucid ambassador at Chandragupta's court (classical tradition). (✅ owner 14 §10.2; ✅ owner 14 basic §1)
**Breakdown:** Ambassador role (✅ owner 14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2165, ANC-2170
**Trap:** Do not confuse Megasthenes' Seleucid service with a Mauryan official role. (⚠️ standard reading)
**Mains hook:** Use to show diplomacy as a source channel.
**Back:** *"Megasthenes: Seleucid envoy to Pataliputra."*

### Flashcard ANC-2172 — Bindusara: Amitrochates, Amitraghāta and thin evidence
**Front:** Who was Bindusara, and what is the evidentiary status of his reign?
**Definition (learn):** Greek sources call Bindusara Amitrochates, commonly connected with the Sanskrit epithet Amitraghāta ("slayer of foes"). Hellenistic traditions connect his court with Antiochus I and others. Later sources attribute southern expansion and provincial governorships to him; direct inscriptional evidence is thin. Bindusara is not a blank interval, nor a precisely mapped Deccan conquest. (✅ owner 14 §10.3)
**Breakdown:**
- Amitrochates / Amitraghāta (✅ owner 14 §10.3)
- Antiochus I contact (✅ owner 14 §10.3)
- Southern expansion attributed later (✅ owner 14 §10.3)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 297–273/268 BCE (✅ owner 14 §9.2).
**Links:** ANC-2164, ANC-2173
**Trap:** Do not credit Bindusara with a precisely mapped Deccan conquest without evidence (✅ owner 14 §10.3).
**Mains hook:** Use Bindusara to show the limits of the "Ashoka-centred" narrative.
**Back:** *"Bindusara: Amitrochates, slayer of foes, thin in stone."*

### Flashcard ANC-2173 — Ashoka: Devānāmpiya Piyadassi, the name Ashoka and the accession narrative
**Front:** What titles does Ashoka use in his edicts, and what is the status of the violent-succession narrative?
**Definition (learn):** Most edicts use Beloved of the Gods, He of Gracious Mien (Devanampiya Piyadasi). The personal name Ashoka appears in selected Minor Rock Edict versions such as Maski and Gujarra. Later Buddhist narratives describe a violent succession and a cruel early Ashoka; numbers of brothers killed and conversion episodes are moral biography, not contemporary facts. (✅ owner 14 §11.1)
**Breakdown:**
- Devānāṁpiya Piyadassi: standard edict title (✅ owner 14 §11.1)
- "Ashoka" name: Maski and Gujarra (✅ owner 14 §11.1)
- Violent accession: later Buddhist memory (✅ owner 14 §11.1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ashoka's reign 273–232 BCE (R.S. Sharma); accession/consecration chronology varies (✅ owner 14). Wikipedia "Edicts of Ashoka" confirms the Maski inscription identifies Devānāṃpiya with Aśoka (found 1915 by C. Beadon, Raichur district, Karnataka) and the Gujarra edict (Datia district, Madhya Pradesh) gives the full title "Devānāṃpiya Piyadasi Asokarāja" (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ANC-2164, ANC-2167, ANC-2197
**Trap:** Ashoka the name is not on most edicts; do not treat "Devānāṁpiya" as a personal name alone (✅ owner 14 §11.1).
**Mains hook:** Use the title to show royal self-presentation.
**Back:** *"Beloved of the Gods, Gracious Mien: most edicts. Ashoka: Maski and Gujarra."*

### Flashcard ANC-2174 — Kalinga war: Major Rock Edict XIII, eighth regnal year, 261 BCE
**Front:** What does Major Rock Edict XIII state about Kalinga, and what are the secure facts?
**Definition (learn):** Major Rock Edict XIII states that Kalinga was conquered in Ashoka's eighth regnal year and gives very large killed, deported and suffering figures. Secure facts: conquest, royal acknowledgement of suffering, and a post-war preference for dhamma-vijaya (conquest by dhamma) in political communication. The figures are royal statement, not audited totals. (✅ owner 14 §11.2; ✅ owner 14 basic mini-timeline 261 BC)
**Breakdown:**
- Eighth regnal year (✅ owner 14 §11.2)
- Killed/deported figures: royal statement (✅ owner 14 §9.2)
- Dhamma-vijaya preference (✅ owner 14 §11.2)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 261 BC (conventional; ✅ owner 14 basic §4). Wikipedia "Edicts of Ashoka" confirms the conquest was in Ashoka's **eighth year** and dates Rock Edict XIII to about his 14th regnal year (c. 256–255 BCE). **Owner-vs-source conflict:** the 261 BCE date for the war is the textbook convention; the edict's own date is later. Keep the two separate (⚠️).
**Links:** ANC-2164, ANC-2175, ANC-2176
**Trap:** "Kalinga war happened before Ashoka's reign" is wrong (✅ owner 14 basic §5).
**Mains hook:** "Link Kalinga, dhamma and inscriptions to the beginnings of public political communication in India." (✅ owner 14 basic §7)
**Back:** *"Kalinga, eighth year, 261: remorse proclaimed, sovereignty kept."*

### Flashcard ANC-2175 — Dhamma-vijaya is not pacifism: retained army, punishment and forest warnings
**Front:** Why must Ashoka's post-Kalinga policy not be read as total pacifism?
**Definition (learn):** The empire, officers, punishments and army continued; Rock Edict XIII warns forest peoples (āṭavikas) that the king retained power to punish; later pillar policy includes justice and reprieve, not abolition of coercion; Ashoka distinguished remorse over conquest from surrender of sovereignty. (✅ owner 14 basic §4; ✅ owner 14 §11.2, §16.2)
**Breakdown:**
- Army retained (✅ owner 14 basic §4)
- Warning to āṭavikas (✅ owner 14 §11.2)
- Pillar justice and reprieve (✅ owner 14 §11.2)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rock Edict XIII (✅ owner 14 advanced §1).
**Links:** ANC-2174, ANC-2176, ANC-2203
**Trap:** "Ashoka became a pacifist and abolished coercion" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §23).
**Mains hook:** Use the limits to argue for "moral language within coercive power".
**Back:** *"Dhamma-vijaya: remorse without surrender; army stays, forests are warned."*

### Flashcard ANC-2176 — Separate Kalinga Edicts at Dhauli and Jaugada
**Front:** What are the Separate Kalinga Edicts, and to whom do they speak?
**Definition (learn):** The Separate Kalinga Edicts at Dhauli and Jaugada address officials and subjects in paternal governance language. Royal intention is not proof of uniform implementation. (✅ owner 14 §11.2)
**Breakdown:**
- Dhauli and Jaugada (Odisha/Kalinga context) (✅ owner 14 §17 high-yield sites)
- Officials and subjects; paternal language (✅ owner 14 §11.2)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated beyond Ashoka.
**Links:** ANC-2174, ANC-2177, ANC-2199
**Trap:** Dhauli and Jaugada are both Odisha; do not confuse with Erragudi (Andhra Pradesh) (✅ owner 14 §17, §25).
**Mains hook:** Use the Kalinga edicts to show the bridge between conquest and dhamma.
**Back:** *"Dhauli and Jaugada: Kalinga's edicts, officials addressed as children."*

### Flashcard ANC-2177 — Imperial geography: inscriptional zones, not a uniform map
**Front:** Which zones does the inscriptional evidence attest, and what is each zone's limit?
**Definition (learn):** Magadhan core (Pāṭaliputra; middle Gangā) — strongest base. North-west (Shahbazgarhi, Mansehra; Kandahar Greek/Aramaic) — frontier languages and scripts, control mediated. Western India (Girnar and Sopara traditions) — coastal routes. Himalayan/Terai (Kalsi, Lumbini, Nigali Sāgar) — foothill routes and pilgrimage. Kalinga (Dhauli, Jaugada) — conquered province with distinct edict versions. Deccan/south (Brahmagiri, Siddapur, Jatinga-Rāmeśvara, Maski, Erragudi) — wide communication, varied direct rule. Tamil polities/Sri Lanka horizon (Chola, Pāṇḍya, Satiyaputra, Keralaputra, Tambapaṇṇi named in edicts) — neighbours, not demonstrated provinces. Forest/frontier zones (āṭavika peoples) — negotiation. (✅ owner 14 §12)
**Breakdown:** Eight zones (✅ owner 14 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Findspots are not survey pegs for borders (✅ owner 14 §17).
**Links:** ANC-2178, ANC-2196, ANC-2197, ANC-2203
**Trap:** "Mauryan borders equal a map drawn around edict sites" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §23). Chola, Pāṇḍya etc. are neighbours, not provinces (✅ owner 14 §12).
**Mains hook:** Use zones to argue for layered control.
**Back:** *"Core, north-west, west, Himalaya, Kalinga, Deccan, south-neighbours, forests: zones, not borders."*

### Flashcard ANC-2178 — Provinces, princes and provincial centres: Taxila, Ujjain, Tosali, Suvarṇagiri
**Front:** Which provincial centres are associated with the Mauryan provinces, and who governed them?
**Definition (learn):** Provincial centres conventionally associated with Taxila, Ujjain, Tosali and Suvarṇagiri, often under princes (kumāra/āryaputra) and officials. The empire was divided into provinces, each often under a prince of the royal dynasty. Epigraphic and literary evidence does not prove one standardised provincial template. (✅ owner 14 basic §1; ✅ owner 14 §12)
**Breakdown:**
- Taxila (north-west) (✅ owner 14)
- Ujjain (west) (✅ owner 14)
- Tosali (Kalinga) (⚠️ standard; owner names Tosali as provincial centre)
- Suvarṇagiri (Deccan) (✅ owner 14)
- kumāra/āryaputra (✅ owner 14 §12)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated. Basic mentions Kausambi as an important centre in §4 (✅ owner 14 basic §4).
**Links:** ANC-2177, ANC-2179, ANC-2180
**Trap:** Not every province necessarily had a prince. "Provinces were uniform" is wrong. (✅ owner 14 §13.2)
**Mains hook:** Use provincial centres to show a graded hierarchy.
**Back:** *"Taxila, Ujjain, Tosali, Suvarnagiri: princes or officials, not one template."*

### Flashcard ANC-2179 — Officers: mahāmātra, dharma-mahāmātra, prādeśika, rājūka, yukta, kumāra
**Front:** Which Mauryan officers are attested or discussed, and what are their evidence-led roles?
**Definition (learn):** Mahāmātra: broad high official, specialised groups appear in edicts. Dharma-mahāmātra: appointed for dhamma-related welfare, social and religious concerns (royal policy office, not Buddhist clergy). Prādeśika: touring/field officer in Rock Edict III context. Rājūka: responsibilities involving people, welfare, justice and perhaps land measurement. Yukta: subordinate or executive-accounting official in touring and reporting contexts. Kumāra/āryaputra: royal prince or provincial authority. (✅ owner 14 §13.2)
**Breakdown:** Six officer categories (✅ owner 14 §13.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rock Edict III context for prādeśika (✅ owner 14 §13.2).
**Links:** ANC-2180, ANC-2181, ANC-2184, ANC-2194
**Trap:** Dharma-mahāmātra is not Buddhist clergy (✅ owner 14 §13.2). Prādeśika is not a modern district magistrate (✅ owner 14 §13.2).
**Mains hook:** Use the officer list to show specialisation without a modern ranking.
**Back:** *"Mahamatra high, dharma-mahamatra for welfare, pradeshika tours, rajuka judges, yukta reports, kumara rules."*

### Flashcard ANC-2180 — PYQ 2025 GS-I Q19: Prādeśika, Rājūka and Yukta at the district level
**Front:** What does the 2025 Prelims item on prādeśika, rājūka and yukta test, and what is the official key direction?
**Definition (learn):** PYQ 2025 Q19 tests Ashokan officers Prādeśika, Rājūka and Yukta. The owner records the official Set-A key as grouping these offices at the district level. Historical explanation must preserve their distinct functions and varying contexts; do not make them identical modern posts. The official key is recorded in owner 14 as available locally; the answer letter is not recorded here. (✅ owner 14 §13.2, §25; ✅ owner 14 2024–2025 integration)
**Breakdown:**
- Key direction: district-level administration (✅ owner 14 §13.2)
- Distinct functions preserved (✅ owner 14 §13.2)
- Context-dependent (✅ owner 14 §13.2)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Prelims GS-I Q19 (✅ owner 14 2024–2025 integration).
**Links:** ANC-2179, ANC-2181
**Trap:** Do not read "district level" as "identical modern district magistrates". (✅ owner 14 §13.2)
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains item; a Prelims officers' trap.
**Back:** *"Pradeshika, rajuka, yukta: district-level in the key, distinct in function."*

### Flashcard ANC-2181 — Samāhartṛ and sannidhātṛ: revenue assessor and treasury custodian (prescriptive)
**Front:** What do samāhartṛ and sannidhātṛ denote, and why are they not automatically verified portfolios?
**Definition (learn):** In the Arthashastra, the samāhartṛ is the officer of revenue assessment and collection; the sannidhātṛ is the custodian of the treasury and stores. In the basic owner's list, samaharta is the highest officer in charge of assessment and the sannidhata the custodian of the state treasury. These are prescriptive categories, not verified portfolios across the empire. (✅ owner 14 basic §1, §2; ✅ owner 14 §13.1)
**Breakdown:**
- Samāhartṛ: assessment and collection (✅ owner 14 §13.1)
- Sannidhātṛ: treasury and store (✅ owner 14 §13.1)
- Prescriptive, not uniform (✅ owner 14 §13.1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2166, ANC-2179, ANC-2142
**Trap:** Basic §1 calls samaharta "the highest officer in charge of assessment" — this is an owner claim, not an empire-wide verified rank. ⚠️ (owner 14 basic §1 vs §13.1)
**Mains hook:** Use the categories to show prescriptive versus practice.
**Back:** *"Samahartr assesses, sannidhatr keeps the treasury: the Arthashastra's categories."*

### Flashcard ANC-2182 — King, council (mantri-pariṣad) and officials
**Front:** How did kingship depend on counsellors and officials in the Mauryan state?
**Definition (learn):** The king was commander, judge, patron and source of appointments, but depended on ministers, the council (mantri-pariṣad), officials, scribes, messengers, provincial elites and local intermediaries. (✅ owner 14 §13.1)
**Breakdown:**
- King: commander, judge, patron, appointer (✅ owner 14 §13.1)
- Council: mantri-pariṣad (✅ owner 14 §13.1)
- Intermediaries: provincial elites, local leaders (✅ owner 14 §13.1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2179, ANC-2203
**Trap:** Do not present the king as absolute in every region. (✅ owner 14 §20)
**Mains hook:** Use the council to show a layered court.
**Back:** *"King commands, council counsels, officials and local elites carry it out."*

### Flashcard ANC-2183 — Espionage, reporting and communication: Arthashastra versus edicts
**Front:** What do the Arthashastra and the edicts each attest about espionage and communication?
**Definition (learn):** Espionage networks, secret agents and royal surveillance are most fully elaborated in the Arthashastra, a theory of statecraft, not an operational record. Ashokan edicts more securely attest tours, reports, messengers and concern that business be brought to the king. (✅ owner 14 §13.3)
**Breakdown:**
- Arthashastra: espionage elaborated (✅ owner 14 §13.3)
- Edicts: tours, reports, messengers (✅ owner 14 §13.3)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2166, ANC-2167, ANC-2179
**Trap:** Do not present Arthashastra espionage as a documented Mauryan operation. (✅ owner 14 §13.3)
**Mains hook:** Use the two sources to separate theory from practice.
**Back:** *"Arthashastra spies in theory; edicts show tours and reports in practice."*

### Flashcard ANC-2184 — Justice: rājūkas, Pillar Edict IV and paternal reprieve
**Front:** What judicial role did rājūkas have, and what does Pillar Edict IV show?
**Definition (learn):** Ashoka entrusted rājūkas with significant judicial and welfare responsibility. Pillar Edict IV's concern with uniformity and a reprieve for condemned prisoners shows paternal justice within retained coercive power. Different legal statuses in Arthashastra provisions are normative and do not prove uniform enforcement. (✅ owner 14 §13.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Rājūka judicial and welfare role (✅ owner 14 §13.4)
- Pillar Edict IV: uniformity; reprieve (✅ owner 14 §13.4)
- Retained coercion (✅ owner 14 §13.4)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2179, ANC-2175
**Trap:** Reprieve does not mean abolition of punishment. (✅ owner 14 §11.2)
**Mains hook:** Use Pillar Edict IV to show justice as paternal.
**Back:** *"Rajuka judges, Pillar IV reprieves: justice paternal, coercion kept."*

### Flashcard ANC-2185 — Municipal and village administration: Pāṭaliputra's six committees and village headmen
**Front:** What does Megasthenes say about Pāṭaliputra's administration, and how far can it be generalised?
**Definition (learn):** Megasthenes' six committees of five members for Pāṭaliputra survive only through later quotations and should not be generalised to all cities. Villages were mediated through headmen, cultivators, officials and fiscal networks; local institutions and regional variation remain poorly documented. (✅ owner 14 §13.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Six committees of five (later quotations) (✅ owner 14 §13.5)
- Village: headmen and fiscal mediation (✅ owner 14 §13.5)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2165, ANC-2134
**Trap:** Do not generalise the six committees to all cities. (✅ owner 14 §13.5)
**Mains hook:** Use the committees to show the difference between a city example and empire-wide practice.
**Back:** *"Six committees of five for Pataliputra: a Greek quotation, not a template for all towns."*

### Flashcard ANC-2186 — Army: classical description, plausible fiscal-military capacity, unaudited numbers
**Front:** What does the evidence say about the Mauryan army, and what must not be stated?
**Definition (learn):** Classical writers describe large infantry, cavalry, chariot and elephant forces and administrative boards. A major fiscal-military capacity is plausible; exact numbers and committee structure are not audited Mauryan statistics. Fortifications and long-service troops indicate organised coercive capacity. (✅ owner 14 §13.6; ✅ owner 14 basic §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Four arms described by classical writers (✅ owner 14 §13.6)
- Fiscal-military capacity plausible (✅ owner 14 §13.6)
- Numbers not audited (✅ owner 14 §13.6)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2138, ANC-2165, ANC-2170
**Trap:** Do not reproduce army figures as fact (✅ owner 14 §13.6, §10.1).
**Mains hook:** Use the army as evidence of fiscal capacity, not of exact size.
**Back:** *"Infantry, cavalry, chariots, elephants: plausible force, unaudited numbers."*

### Flashcard ANC-2187 — Political economy: crown land (sīta), produce share (bhāga) and the Sudarśana lake (Junāgaḍh)
**Front:** What do the Arthashastra's revenue terms and the Junāgaḍh inscription show about the economy?
**Definition (learn):** Arthashastra discusses crown land (sīta), produce share (bhāga), tribute/taxes, tolls, fines and labour obligations; these are prescriptive and may contain layers. The later Junāgaḍh inscription recalls Puṣyagupta under Chandragupta and Tuṣāspha under Ashoka in relation to the Sudarśana lake; it is retrospective evidence of state-supported irrigation, not a complete irrigation policy. (✅ owner 14 §14.1)
**Breakdown:**
- sīta; bhāga; taxes; tolls; fines; labour (✅ owner 14 §14.1)
- Junāgaḍh: Puṣyagupta; Tuṣāspha; Sudarśana (✅ owner 14 §14.1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Junāgaḍh inscription is later (✅ owner 14 §14.1).
**Links:** ANC-2166, ANC-2142, ANC-2188
**Trap:** Do not treat the Sudarśana lake as proof of empire-wide irrigation policy (✅ owner 14 §14.1).
**Mains hook:** Use the lake to show state irrigation as a regional instance.
**Back:** *"Sita and bhaga in theory; Sudarshana lake, retrospective and local."*

### Flashcard ANC-2188 — Coinage: punch-marked series without royal names
**Front:** What do punch-marked coins and hoards show, and why is "Mauryan imperial currency" unsafe?
**Definition (learn):** Punch-marked coins and hoards indicate circulation and fiscal or market transactions. They generally lack royal names; "Mauryan imperial currency" cannot be assumed for every series or find. Payments in kind and local exchange continued. (✅ owner 14 §14.3)
**Breakdown:**
- Circulation and fiscal/market use (✅ owner 14 §14.3)
- No royal names (✅ owner 14 §14.3)
- Kind payments continued (✅ owner 14 §14.3)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2136, ANC-2091, ANC-2109
**Trap:** "All coins were Mauryan imperial coins" is wrong. (✅ owner 14 §14.3)
**Mains hook:** Use coins to show monetary integration without royal credit.
**Back:** *"Punch-marked, unnamed, barter still alive: currency without a royal signature."*

### Flashcard ANC-2189 — State control: Arthashastra model versus uneven reality
**Front:** Did the Mauryan state monopolise mines, forests, salt, trade and workshops?
**Definition (learn):** The Arthashastra describes superintendents, mines, forests, salt, trade, workshops and regulation. Treat this as a strong model of political economy, not proof of a universal Mauryan state monopoly or a modern command economy. Control was likely strongest near metropolitan and provincial centres and key resources and routes. (✅ owner 14 §14.4; ✅ owner 14 §23)
**Breakdown:**
- Model: superintendents and regulation (✅ owner 14 §14.4)
- Strongest control: centres, key resources, routes (✅ owner 14 §14.4)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2166, ANC-2188, ANC-2203
**Trap:** "All mines, forests, workshops and trade were state monopolies" is wrong. (✅ owner 14 §23)
**Mains hook:** Use the model/practice split to discuss the command-economy caricature.
**Back:** *"Superintendents in the book, uneven control on the ground."*

### Flashcard ANC-2190 — Megasthenes' seven groups and the slavery contradiction
**Front:** How do Megasthenes' seven groups and his no-slavery claim relate to the four-varna model and to dāsa/dāsī evidence?
**Definition (learn):** Megasthenes' seven occupational or social groups do not map neatly onto the four varna model. His statement that India lacked slavery conflicts with Arthashastra, Buddhist/Jain and other evidence for dāsa/dāsī, hired labour, debt and dependence. The contradiction reveals outsider categories and translation, not a choice between one wholly true and one false source. (✅ owner 14 §15.1)
**Breakdown:**
- Seven groups vs four varnas (✅ owner 14 §15.1)
- No-slavery claim vs dependency evidence (✅ owner 14 §15.1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2165, ANC-2147, ANC-2191
**Trap:** "Megasthenes proves there was no slavery" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §23).
**Mains hook:** Use the contradiction to show outsider category problems.
**Back:** *"Seven groups, no slaves? The outsider's claim meets the insider's dependence."*

### Flashcard ANC-2191 — PYQ 2022 Q60: Arthashastra slavery and legal provisions (text-based keyed route)
**Front:** Which slavery statements does the 2022 Arthashastra item accept, and what does the owner say about frequency?
**Definition (learn):** For the 2022 PYQ, the text-based keyed interpretation accepts: (a) a female slave bearing her master's son becomes legally free; and (b) that son has the legal status of the master's son. The judicial-punishment statement is not accepted in the question's form. This does not prove empire-wide frequency or implementation. (✅ owner 14 §15.3, §25)
**Breakdown:**
- Accepted: female slave's child by master becomes free; son has master's son's status (✅ owner 14 §15.3)
- Not accepted: judicial-punishment statement as worded (✅ owner 14 §15.3)
- Prescription ≠ practice (✅ owner 14 §15.3)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Prelims GS-I Q60 (✅ owner 14 §25). The owner states the key interpretation as text-based.
**Links:** ANC-2147, ANC-2190, ANC-2166
**Trap:** Do not extend the Arthashastra's legal provisions to empire-wide practice. (✅ owner 14 §15.3)
**Mains hook:** Use the example to illustrate statute versus implementation.
**Back:** *"Arthashastra law: the slave's son becomes free; practice stays unproven."*

### Flashcard ANC-2192 — Women and household power: the Queen's Edict and Karuvaki
**Front:** What does the Queen's Edict show about royal women's patronage?
**Definition (learn):** Sources mention queens, palace women, female guards in Greek tradition, workers, donors and renunciants. The Queen's Edict names Karuvaki and records her gifts, showing visible royal female patronage. Arthashastra rules concerning women are prescriptive. Agency coexisted with patriarchal marriage, inheritance, labour and legal constraints. (✅ owner 14 §15.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Queen's Edict: Karuvaki (✅ owner 14 §15.4)
- Female guards: Greek tradition (✅ owner 14 §15.4)
- Constraints: patriarchal marriage, inheritance (✅ owner 14 §15.4)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2152, ANC-2015
**Trap:** Do not generalise queenly patronage to all women's status. (✅ owner 14 §15.4)
**Mains hook:** Use the Queen's Edict to show visibility within constraint.
**Back:** *"Karuvaki's gifts on the edict: visible patronage, patriarchy intact."*

### Flashcard ANC-2193 — Ashokan dhamma: content, public vocabulary and limits
**Front:** What did Ashokan dhamma include, and what was it not?
**Definition (learn):** Ashoka's public dhamma included respect for parents, elders, Brāhmaṇas and Śramaṇas; generosity and proper conduct; humane treatment of servants and dependants; restraint in killing and animal slaughter; moderation in ceremonies; truth, purity and self-examination; restraint in praising one's own sect and condemning others; welfare and concern for subjects. It was connected to Ashoka's personal Buddhism, broader than Buddhist doctrine in its public vocabulary, an instrument of imperial ethical communication, not a modern secular constitution, and not proof of social harmony everywhere. (✅ owner 14 §16)
**Breakdown:** Eight content items (✅ owner 14 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2194, ANC-2195, ANC-2198, ANC-2208
**Trap:** "Dhamma was simply Buddhism or modern secularism" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §23).
**Mains hook:** "Ashoka transformed dhamma from religious discourse into political language." Discuss (✅ owner 14 advanced §7).
**Back:** *"Respect, generosity, dependants, restraint, moderation, truth, tolerance, welfare: public dhamma, not Buddhism alone."*

### Flashcard ANC-2194 — Dhamma machinery: dharma-mahāmātras, tours, Rock Edicts II and XII
**Front:** What administrative machinery supported dhamma, and what do Rock Edicts II and XII add?
**Definition (learn):** Dharma-mahāmātras were appointed after a stated regnal interval to work among social and religious groups. Officers undertook periodic dhamma tours. Rock Edict II speaks of medical provision and useful plants and wells for humans and animals. Rock Edict XII urges sectarian restraint and listening, not mere passive toleration. Inscriptions, messengers and local versions adapted communication to audiences. (✅ owner 14 §16.1)
**Breakdown:**
- Dharma-mahāmātras (✅ owner 14 §16.1)
- Tours (✅ owner 14 §16.1)
- Rock Edict II: medical provision, plants, wells (✅ owner 14 §16.1)
- Rock Edict XII: restraint and listening (✅ owner 14 §16.1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated beyond regnal intervals.
**Links:** ANC-2179, ANC-2198, ANC-2193
**Trap:** Dharma-mahāmātra is not a clergy (✅ owner 14 §13.2).
**Mains hook:** Use the machinery to show dhamma as administrative policy.
**Back:** *"Dharma officers tour, Rock II heals, Rock XII listens."*

### Flashcard ANC-2195 — Animal policy: restricted killing, not a total ban
**Front:** What did Ashoka restrict regarding animals, and what did he not do?
**Definition (learn):** Ashoka restricted sacrifice, royal-kitchen killing and the killing of named animals or on named days. He did not institute a simple total empire-wide ban on meat, hunting, punishment or warfare. Policy combined moral reduction, symbolic royal example and practical exceptions. (✅ owner 14 §16.2)
**Breakdown:**
- Restricted: sacrifice; royal-kitchen killing; named animals; named days (✅ owner 14 §16.2)
- Not banned: meat, hunting, punishment, warfare (✅ owner 14 §16.2)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2193, ANC-2175
**Trap:** "Ashoka banned meat and hunting empire-wide" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §16.2).
**Mains hook:** Use the animal policy to show partial and symbolic restriction.
**Back:** *"Restrict the kitchen, the sacrifice, named days: not a total ban."*

### Flashcard ANC-2196 — Scripts and languages: Brāhmī, Kharoṣṭhī, Greek and Aramaic
**Front:** Which scripts and languages do the Ashokan edicts use, and where?
**Definition (learn):** Most subcontinental edicts use Brāhmī with Prakrit varieties; the north-west uses Kharoṣṭhī and Prakrit at Shahbazgarhi and Mansehra (frontier script linked to Aramaic); Afghanistan has Greek and Aramaic versions (multilingual imperial audience). Categories: Major Rock, Minor Rock, Separate Rock, Major Pillar and Minor Pillar Edicts. No site contains one complete master set. (✅ owner 14 §17)
**Breakdown:**
- Brāhmī + Prakrit (most) (✅ owner 14 §17)
- Kharoṣṭhī + Prakrit: Shahbazgarhi, Mansehra (✅ owner 14 §17)
- Greek and Aramaic: Afghanistan (Kandahar) (✅ owner 14 §17, §12)
- Five categories (✅ owner 14 §17)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2107, ANC-2108, ANC-2177, ANC-2197
**Trap:** "Kharoṣṭhī is a south-Indian script" is wrong (✅ owner 12 §22). Findspots are not survey pegs (✅ owner 14 §17).
**Mains hook:** Use scripts as a map of communication, not of borders.
**Back:** *"Brahmi for most, Kharoshthi for the north-west, Greek and Aramaic in Afghanistan."*

### Flashcard ANC-2197 — High-yield edict sites: Dhauli, Jaugaḍa, Erragudi, Kalsi, Shāhbāzgarhī, Mānsehrā, Girnār, Maski and Gujarra, Lumbinī and Nigālī Sāgar
**Front:** Which edict sites are high-yield, and what does each represent?
**Definition (learn):** Dhauli and Jaugaḍa: Odisha/Kaliṅga context. Erragudi/Yerragudi: Andhra Pradesh. Kalsi: Uttarakhand. Shāhbāzgarhī and Mānsehrā: north-west, Kharoṣṭhī. Girnār: Gujarat. Maski/Gujarra: personal-name evidence (Minor Rock Edicts). Lumbinī and Nigālī Sāgar: pilgrimage and Terai pillars. (✅ owner 14 §17)
**Breakdown:** Seven site groups (✅ owner 14 §17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2173, ANC-2176, ANC-2177, ANC-2199, ANC-2200
**Trap:** Do not map Dhauli and Jaugaḍa to different states; both are Odisha. Kalsi is Uttarakhand, not Odisha (✅ owner 14 §25).
**Mains hook:** Use the sites to show the zones.
**Back:** *"Dhauli, Jaugada: Odisha. Erragudi: Andhra. Kalsi: Uttarakhand. Girnar: Gujarat. Maski: the name."*

### Flashcard ANC-2198 — PYQ 2020 Q30: Major Rock Edict XII on sectarian self-praise
**Front:** What is the Ashokan inscription tested in PYQ 2020 Q30, and how should concord be described?
**Definition (learn):** The inscription against sectarian self-praise and denunciation is Major Rock Edict XII. Concord is not modern secularism, and the edict is not proof of social harmony. Its message: restraint in praising one's own sect and condemning others; listening. (✅ owner 14 §25; ✅ owner 14 §16.1)
**Breakdown:**
- Major Rock Edict XII (✅ owner 14 §25)
- Restraint and listening, not passive toleration (✅ owner 14 §16.1)
- Not modern secularism (✅ owner 14 §25)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Prelims GS-I Q30; the official key is not held locally (✅ owner 14 2018–2023 integration).
**Links:** ANC-2193, ANC-2194
**Trap:** "Edict proves social harmony" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §25).
**Mains hook:** Use Rock Edict XII to argue for pluralist ethics with a limit.
**Back:** *"Rock XII: praise no sect alone, listen to all."*

### Flashcard ANC-2199 — PYQ 2022 Q91: Major Rock Edict site–state pairs
**Front:** Which site–state pairs in the 2022 Major Rock Edict item are correct?
**Definition (learn):** Dhauli–Odisha and Erragudi–Andhra Pradesh are correct. Jaugada is in Odisha and Kalsi is in Uttarakhand; the owner records that only two pairs were correct. The official key is not recorded in the owner's 2018–2023 block (no key held locally). (✅ owner 14 §25; ✅ owner 14 2018–2023 integration)
**Breakdown:**
- Dhauli–Odisha: correct (✅ owner 14 §25)
- Erragudi–Andhra Pradesh: correct (✅ owner 14 §25)
- Jaugada–Odisha and Kalsi–Uttarakhand: correct facts, owner route as "only two pairs" (✅ owner 14 §25)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Prelims GS-I Q91; key not held locally. Wikipedia "Edicts of Ashoka" places Dhauli, Jaugada (two edicts in Odisha), Yerragudi, Khalsi, Shahbazgarhi, Mansehra and Girnar among the Major Rock Edict sites, and the Kandahar Greek edict in Afghanistan (✅ Wikipedia; the Kalsi site is Uttarakhand per owner 14 §17).
**Links:** ANC-2197, ANC-2176
**Trap:** The "only two pairs" count is the owner's; check it against the question's pairs before answering. ⚠️
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains item; a site-state matching trap.
**Back:** *"Dhauli, Erragudi right; Jaugada and Kalsi sit in Odisha and Uttarakhand."*

### Flashcard ANC-2200 — PYQ 2019 Q8: Ranyo/Raya Ashoka portrait inscription at Kanaganahalli
**Front:** What does the 2019 Prelims item on the Ashoka stone portrait test, and what caution applies?
**Definition (learn):** The item tests the "Raya/Ranyo Ashoka" portrait inscription at Kanaganahalli. The owner records it as a later Buddhist sculptural memory, not a lifetime portrait. The key is inferred because the official local key is unavailable; treat the answer as not officially verified. (✅ owner 14 §25)
**Breakdown:**
- Kanaganahalli (✅ owner 14 §25)
- Later sculptural memory (✅ owner 14 §25)
- Key inferred; unavailable locally (✅ owner 14 §25)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Prelims GS-I Q8 (✅ owner 14 2018–2023 integration). Wikipedia "Edicts of Ashoka" notes a Kanaganahalli relief inscribed "Raya Asoka", which supports identifying the Ashoka figure in the Sanchi relief showing him with two queens (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** ANC-2173, ANC-2197
**Trap:** Do not treat the portrait as a lifetime likeness. (✅ owner 14 §25)
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains item; an iconography trap.
**Back:** *"Kanaganahalli Ashoka: memory in relief, not a face from life."*

### Flashcard ANC-2201 — Ashoka's Buddhism, the Third Council and missions: chronicles versus edicts
**Front:** Which evidence supports Ashoka's Buddhist commitment, and which Buddhist claims are chronicle-based?
**Definition (learn):** Pilgrimage and Lumbinī/Nigālī Sāgar records; the Bairat/Bhābru Edict's recommendation of Buddhist texts; concern with sangha discipline in schism edicts; and Buddhist memory of patronage support his commitment. The Third Buddhist Council, Moggaliputta Tissa and missions associated with Mahinda and Sanghamitrā are chiefly preserved in Sri Lankan chronicles and later traditions; edicts attest a wide dhamma horizon and Hellenistic rulers but not every council and mission detail. Royal patronage did not create Buddhism or replace lay and monastic networks. (✅ owner 14 §18; ✅ Wikipedia "Buddhist councils" for the Third Council)
**Breakdown:**
- Edict-supported: Lumbinī, Nigālī Sāgar, Bairat/Bhābru, schism edicts (✅ owner 14 §18)
- Chronicle-based: Third Council details; Mahinda; Sanghamitrā (✅ owner 14 §18)
- Royal patronage not sufficient cause (✅ owner 14 §18)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Third Council in Ashoka's reign (✅ Wikipedia; see ANC-2044).
**Links:** ANC-2044, ANC-2056, ANC-2055, ANC-2168
**Trap:** "Third Council and missions are confirmed in every detail by edicts" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §23).
**Mains hook:** Use the split to argue for royal patronage without monocausal credit.
**Back:** *"Edicts show the sangha cared for; chronicles tell the Third Council and missions."*

### Flashcard ANC-2202 — Mauryan art and architecture as historical bridge: pillars, Sarnath, stūpas, Barābar and polish
**Front:** Which Mauryan monuments are historically useful, and what is the caution for each?
**Definition (learn):** Monolithic pillars and capitals (quarrying, transport, polish, royal message); the Sārnāth lion capital (later national memory of royal-Buddhist sovereignty; meaning layered); stūpas (patronage, relic and pilgrimage networks; "84,000 stūpas" is a symbolic tradition); Barābar caves (Ashoka and Dasharatha inscriptions and gifts to Ājīvikas; not every polished cave was Buddhist); Pāṭaliputra/Kumrahar (wooden and pillared urban and court architecture; phases debated); Mauryan polish (technique and origin debate, including Achaemenid parallels and indigenous development). (✅ owner 14 §19)
**Breakdown:** Six forms with cautions (✅ owner 14 §19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Barābar: Ashoka and Dasharatha inscriptions (✅ owner 14 §19, §21).
**Links:** ANC-2108, ANC-2077, ANC-2055, ANC-2204
**Trap:** "84,000 stūpas" is not an audited count. "Mauryan art is wholly Persian" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §23). Detailed iconography belongs to Art and Culture.
**Mains hook:** Use monuments as evidence of resource mobilisation and communication.
**Back:** *"Pillars, lion, stupas, caves, Kumrahar, polish: monuments of power and piety."*

### Flashcard ANC-2203 — The centralisation debate: strong capacity versus differentiated control
**Front:** What evidence supports strong central capacity, and what supports differentiated control? What is the judgement?
**Definition (learn):** Strong capacity: large territorial claims and provincial centres; high officials, tours, reporting and inscriptional communication; fiscal-military and road/resource models; royal intervention in justice, religion, welfare and frontier policy; standardised pillars, edicts and coin circulation. Differentiated control: distance, ecology and communication limits; regional scripts and edict variants; princes, local elites and frontier peoples; inscriptions focused on ethics not routine administration; prescriptive or external texts as main administrative evidence; uneven archaeology and post-Ashoka reassertion. Judgement: a powerful, layered empire with a metropolitan core, provincial nodes, strategic corridors and mediated frontiers, neither a modern unitary bureaucracy nor a symbolic empire. (✅ owner 14 §20)
**Breakdown:** Two lists and one judgement (✅ owner 14 §20).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2163, ANC-2177, ANC-2182, ANC-2189
**Trap:** Do not choose "fully centralised" or "merely symbolic". (✅ owner 14 §20)
**Mains hook:** "Assess whether the Mauryan Empire was a highly centralised state or a layered empire with varied control." (✅ owner 14 §7)
**Back:** *"Core strong, provinces mediated, frontiers negotiated: layered, not unitary."*

### Flashcard ANC-2204 — Succession, decline and the end of the dynasty (Bṛhadratha and Puṣyamitra)
**Front:** Who succeeded Ashoka, what does tradition say about the end of the dynasty, and how is decline explained?
**Definition (learn):** Later sources disagree over rulers and reign lengths after Ashoka. Dasharatha is epigraphically associated with cave dedications; names such as Kuṇāla, Samprati, Śālisuka, Devavarman and Śatadhanvan appear in varying traditions. Puranic tradition makes Bṛhadratha the last Maurya, killed by his general Puṣyamitra, conventionally c. 187/185 BCE. Decline was regional and multicausal: disputed succession; provincial autonomy; fiscal-military and communication costs; uneven control; changing trade and revenue without proof of total collapse; north-western pressures; institutional fragmentation. (✅ owner 14 §21)
**Breakdown:**
- Dasharatha: cave dedications (✅ owner 14 §21)
- Bṛhadratha killed by Puṣyamitra (Puranic) (✅ owner 14 §21)
- Seven causal strands (✅ owner 14 §21)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 187/185 BCE (✅ owner 14 §21, §9.2).
**Links:** ANC-2164, ANC-2205, ANC-2206, 
**Trap:** "Brihadratha's death explains the whole imperial collapse" is wrong (✅ owner 14 §23). "Pushyamitra's identity proves a Brahmanical reaction" is not proven (✅ owner 14 §21).
**Mains hook:** "Rank the mechanisms of Mauryan decline and reject single-cause theses." (✅ owner 14 §24)
**Back:** *"Brihadratha ends the line at 187; decline is many strands, not one blow."*

### Flashcard ANC-2205 — Legacy with limits
**Front:** What did the Mauryas leave behind, and what does "legacy" not mean?
**Definition (learn):** Unprecedented surviving royal inscriptional communication; a major experiment in subcontinental imperial integration; durable memory of ethical kingship and Ashoka; infrastructure, routes and regional political connections; monumental stone, caves, pillars and stūpa patronage; later Buddhist and political appropriations. Legacy does not mean unbroken institutions, uniform cultural homogenisation or modern nationalism. (✅ owner 14 §22)
**Breakdown:** Six legacies, one exclusion list (✅ owner 14 §22).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2167, ANC-2202, ANC-2204
**Trap:** Do not read the Mauryan legacy as modern nationalism (✅ owner 14 §22).
**Mains hook:** Use the legacy list to frame a balanced conclusion.
**Back:** *"Inscriptions, roads, pillars, memory: a legacy with limits, not unity or nationalism."*

### Flashcard ANC-2206 — Mauryan trap register
**Front:** Which unsafe claims about the Mauryan Empire must be corrected?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's register includes: the Arthashastra is Chandragupta's manual; Megasthenes is neutral and his fragments agree; Mauryan borders equal edict sites; the empire was uniformly centralised; Ashoka became a pacifist; dhamma was simply Buddhism or secularism; the Third Council and missions are confirmed in every detail; all mines, forests, workshops and trade were state monopolies; Megasthenes proves no slavery; Mauryan art is wholly Persian or wholly isolated; pacifism, Brahmanical reaction or weak successors caused decline; Brihadratha's death explains collapse. (✅ owner 14 §23)
**Breakdown:** Twelve claims with corrections (✅ owner 14 §23).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed.
**Links:** ANC-2166, ANC-2165, ANC-2175, ANC-2193, ANC-2189, ANC-2204
**Trap:** Telltale words: "manual", "neutral", "uniform", "pacifist", "all", "every", "caused", "explains".
**Mains hook:** Use corrections to qualify any Mauryan answer.
**Back:** *"Manual, neutral, uniform, pacifist, all, every, caused, explains: the Mauryan red flags."*

### Flashcard ANC-2207 — Answer architecture: Mauryan questions (10, 15, 20 marks)
**Front:** What answer skeletons does the owner give for Mauryan questions?
**Definition (learn):** 10 marks/150 words: define demand and evidence class; three named sources, sites or offices; one regional or source limit; two-line graded verdict. 15 marks/250 words: source matrix → ruler/chronology → institution/economy or dhamma mechanism → regional evidence → counterpoint → conclusion. 20 marks/300 words: for the state, sources → imperial geography → institutions → revenue/army/economy → society → regional variation → centralisation debate → verdict; for Ashoka, accession caution → Kalinga → dhamma content → machinery → Buddhism/public policy distinction → coercion limit → legacy; for decline, succession evidence → mechanisms → rejected single-factor theses → multicausal judgement. Paragraph: claim → named evidence → inference → limitation. (✅ owner 14 §24)
**Breakdown:** Three skeletons (✅ owner 14 §24).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Word limits as stated.
**Links:** ANC-2163, ANC-2203, ANC-2206
**Trap:** Do not present the Mauryas as either a modern bureaucracy or symbolic empire.
**Mains hook:** "Discuss the Ashoka's use of moral language in statecraft." (✅ owner 14 §7)
**Back:** *"Sources, geography, institutions, revenue, society, limits, verdict."*

### Flashcard ANC-2208 — Advanced thesis: Ashoka's dhamma as imperial ideology and political language
**Front:** How does the advanced owner read Ashoka's dhamma, and why is it not simply religious discourse?
**Definition (learn):** Ashoka's dhamma was rooted in personal Buddhist faith but functioned as an imperial ideology with his strong personal stamp; he made dhamma/dharma a central political and social issue, not merely a religious term. His edicts are the richest source for his political ideas but mainly discuss dhamma and only incidentally administration. (✅ owner 14 advanced §1, §2)
**Breakdown:**
- Personal faith → imperial ideology (✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
- Political and social term, not only religious (✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
- Edicts primarily on dhamma (✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2193, ANC-2198, ANC-2209
**Trap:** "Dhamma was only personal Buddhist preaching" is wrong (✅ owner 14 advanced §5).
**Mains hook:** "Ashoka transformed dhamma from religious discourse into political language. Discuss." (✅ owner 14 advanced §7)
**Back:** *"Faith made policy: dhamma as the empire's public language."*

### Flashcard ANC-2209 — Advanced source comparison: inscriptions, Arthashastra and Megasthenes
**Front:** How do the three main sources differ in kind, and what is each one's use?
**Definition (learn):** Inscriptions reveal royal ideology; Arthashastra theorises statecraft (sophisticated but date and fit debated); Megasthenes gives an external court view (valuable but fragmentary through later quotations). Divergences must be interpreted carefully. (✅ owner 14 advanced §1, §2, §7)
**Breakdown:**
- Inscriptions: royal ideology (✅ owner 14 advanced §7)
- Arthashastra: theory of statecraft (✅ owner 14 advanced §7)
- Megasthenes: external view (✅ owner 14 advanced §7)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated.
**Links:** ANC-2165, ANC-2166, ANC-2167, ANC-2208
**Trap:** Do not treat divergences as errors in one source; treat as genre and purpose differences. (✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
**Mains hook:** Use this comparison as the source paragraph of a Mauryan answer.
**Back:** *"Edicts ideology, Kautilya theory, Megasthenes outsider view: three lenses, one empire."*

### Flashcard ANC-2210 — Advanced: peripatetic governance and officials in motion
**Front:** What does "peripatetic" governance mean for Ashokan administration?
**Definition (learn):** Officials and inspection teams were constantly moving, giving Ashokan governance a peripatetic quality: tours, reports, messengers and inspection, which connected the centre with provinces without implying uniform control. (✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
**Breakdown:**
- Officials on tour (✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
- Reports and messengers (✅ owner 14 §13.3)
- Not uniform control (✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dharma tours (✅ owner 14 §16.1).
**Links:** ANC-2179, ANC-2183, ANC-2194
**Trap:** "Peripatetic" does not mean uniform bureaucracy. (✅ owner 14 advanced §1)
**Mains hook:** Use peripatetic governance to show communication rather than standardisation.
**Back:** *"Officers on the move: communication yes, uniformity no."*

### Flashcard ANC-2211 — PYQ coverage note for the Mauryan Empire
**Front:** Which PYQs are routed directly to the Mauryan owner, and what is the Mains status?
**Definition (learn):** Direct routes: 2019 Q8 (Kanaganahalli portrait); 2020 Q30 (Rock Edict XII); 2022 Q60 (Arthashastra slavery); 2022 Q91 (Major Rock Edict site-state pairs); 2025 Q19 (prādeśika, rājūka and yukta). No direct Mains PYQ is routed to Topic 14; adjacent stupa and rock-cut questions remain with Indian Art and Culture. (✅ owner 14 §25; ✅ owner 14 2024–2025 integration)
**Breakdown:** Five direct routes; no direct Mains route (✅ owner 14 §25).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Keys: 2025 Q19 official Set-A key (district level) recorded; 2019 key inferred; 2022 keys per owner text route (✅ owner 14 §25).
**Links:** ANC-2180, ANC-2191, ANC-2198, ANC-2199, ANC-2200
**Trap:** Do not invent a Mains route for the Mauryan owner. (✅ owner 14 §25)
**Mains hook:** Use the Mauryan practice to build Mains answers from the layered-empire thesis.
**Back:** *"Five Prelims routes, no Mains route: study the empire's layers."*

## ANCIENT INDIAN HISTORY — Part 3: Post-Maurya, Satavahanas, Sangam, Guptas and Harsha

### GROUP — Significance and Decline of Maurya Rule (basic owner 15; advanced owner 15)

### Flashcard ANC-3001 — Concept: Significance and decline of Mauryan rule — the core theme
**Front:** What is the core idea of Topic 15 (Significance and Decline of Maurya Rule), and why must significance and decline be judged separately?
**Definition (learn):** The Mauryan empire mattered for its scale, state capacity, inscriptional communication, monumental stone art and material connections. Its fall was not a single event: dynastic fall at Pataliputra, imperial fragmentation of provinces and continuing regional social-economic life happened at different places and dates.
**Breakdown:**
- Significance: first virtually subcontinental empire with inscriptional evidence (ANC-3010)
- Decline: multi-causal, read model by model with evidence and criticism (ANC-3011 to ANC-3016)
- Three endings to keep apart (ANC-3017)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mauryan rule c. 324/321–187/185 BCE; Ashoka died c. 232 BCE; dynasty ended c. 187/185 BCE with Pushyamitra Shunga. (✅ owner 15 basic; advanced owner 15 mini-timeline)
**Links:** ANC-3002, ANC-3010, ANC-3016, ANC-3017. Topic 14 owns the general Ashoka and Dhamma narrative.
**Trap:** Mauryan importance is not only Ashoka's Dhamma, and the decline is not one-line. Do not give a single-cause answer.
**Mains hook:** "Mauryan significance outlived the Mauryan political fall." Qualify with a combined structural and political account of decline.
**Back:** *"Scale, stone, state and succession: judge the rise and the fall apart."*

### Flashcard ANC-3002 — Chronology ladder: Mauryan rule to Shunga transition
**Front:** Give the dated ladder from the start of Mauryan rule to the Shunga transition, with confidence levels.
**Definition (learn):** A safe ladder uses broad dates for the dynasty and firmer anchors for Ashoka's death and the end of the line. The exact year-by-year contraction cannot be reconstructed.
**Breakdown:**
- c. 324/321 BCE: Mauryan rule begins (broad, owner)
- Ashokan phase: pillars, edicts, Dhamma (inscriptional anchor)
- c. 232 BCE: Ashoka dies (broad)
- Dasharatha: the clearest later Maurya by inscriptions
- c. 187/185 BCE: Brihadratha's death and Pushyamitra's accession; dynastic end
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Start c. 324/321 BCE (⚠️ owner 15 gives this as an approximate range); Ashoka c. 232 BCE (✅ owner 15); end c. 187/185 BCE. (✅ owner 15)
**Links:** ANC-3001, ANC-3003, ANC-3004, ANC-3005. Chronology spine: `00_Master-Chronology.md` (owner reference).
**Trap:** Do not present 232 BCE as the year the empire collapsed. It is the year of Ashoka's death, and decline was gradual and regional.
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to show that the dynastic end was a late, sudden event at the centre while contraction had been unfolding for decades.
**Back:** *"Ashoka dies 232, Shunga takes over 187: the thin years in between."*

### Flashcard ANC-3003 — Post-Ashoka succession: the thin record
**Front:** Who are the later Mauryan rulers named in Puranic, Buddhist and Jain traditions after Ashoka, and why is the sequence unreliable?
**Definition (learn):** After Ashoka's death, king lists vary. Names such as Kunala, Samprati, Salisuka, Devavarman and Satadhanvan appear in different Puranic, Buddhist or Jain sequences, with no single agreed order.
**Breakdown:**
- Kunala, Samprati (Buddhist/Jain memory)
- Salisuka, Devavarman, Satadhanvan (Puranic and other lists)
- Dasharatha: the only one epigraphically attested among the later rulers
- Brihadratha: last Maurya in Puranic and later traditions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ashoka died c. 232 BCE; dynastic end c. 187/185 BCE. Later rulers are known largely from Puranic, Buddhist and Jain accounts. (✅ owner 15 basic and advanced)
**Links:** ANC-3004, ANC-3005, ANC-3015.
**Trap:** Do not treat a Puranic or Buddhist name as a dated, secure ruler without inscriptional or numismatic support.
**Mains hook:** Argue that "weak successors" is only a label until the succession and provincial mechanism is specified; the thin record prevents a precise ruler-by-ruler account.
**Back:** *"Many names, few inscriptions: the later Mauryas are mostly remembered, not documented."*

### Flashcard ANC-3004 — Dasharatha: the only later Maurya with inscriptions
**Front:** Which later Maurya is the clearest epigraphic ruler after Ashoka, and what do his inscriptions show?
**Definition (learn):** Dasharatha is epigraphically attested through cave dedications, the clearest evidence for a later Maurya. His Nagarjuni (Barabar-region) cave dedications show continued Ajivika patronage.
**Breakdown:**
- Dasharatha: direct evidence for at least one later ruler
- Dedications: Ajivika patronage in the Barabar-Nagarjuni hills
- Limit: narrow geographical and religious evidence, not an imperial map
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dasharatha is the only later Maurya known to have issued inscriptions (✅ owner 15 advanced). Barabar cave dedications show Ajivika patronage alongside Buddhist memory (✅ owner 15 basic section 11.7).
**Links:** ANC-3003, ANC-3005, ANC-3015.
**Trap:** "Dasharatha proves imperial control" is wrong. The cave records show a patron, not the extent of the empire.
**Mains hook:** Use the Dasharatha evidence to show how sparse post-Ashoka sources are, and why decline theories must state their evidence.
**Back:** *"Dasharatha alone speaks in stone after Ashoka."*

### Flashcard ANC-3005 — Brihadratha to Pushyamitra: the army-review story
**Front:** How is the end of the Maurya line narrated, and which source carries the army-review story?
**Definition (learn):** Puranic lists name Brihadratha as the last Maurya. The story that his general Pushyamitra killed him at an army review is transmitted in Bana's much later Harshacharita.
**Breakdown:**
- Brihadratha: last Maurya in Puranic and later traditions
- Pushyamitra Shunga: general who takes over at Pataliputra
- Harshacharita: the army-review narrative, written centuries later
- Dynastic end conventionally c. 187/185 BCE
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pushyamitra's dynasty begins c. 187/185 BCE (✅ owner 15 mini-timeline; the date is conventional). The Harshacharita account is later, by Bana. (✅ owner 15 section 12)
**Links:** ANC-3002, ANC-3011, ANC-3015.
**Trap:** A change of dynasty at the centre does not prove an empire-wide Brahmanical revolution, immediate control of all former zones, or systematic persecution of Buddhism.
**Mains hook:** Use the Harshacharita story to show how later memory can moralise a dynastic change; qualify before linking Pushyamitra to a religious reaction.
**Back:** *"Bana's review-ground story: a coup at the centre, not an empire-wide revolution."*

### Flashcard ANC-3006 — Mauryan stone masonry and polish
**Front:** What did the Mauryas contribute to stone architecture and what is the "shining polish" of Ashokan pillars?
**Definition (learn):** The Mauryas expanded quarrying, transport, polishing and stone construction on an unprecedented imperial scale. They did not invent stone working in South Asia. Ashokan pillars are famous for a shining polish, comparable to NBPW polish.
**Breakdown:**
- Stone masonry on a wide scale (imperial scale, not invention)
- Polished monolithic pillars with animal capitals
- Pillars carried edicts and political message
- Megasthenes compared the Pataliputra palace with the splendour of Iran's capital
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mauryan stone masonry on a wide scale; polished Ashokan pillars; Megasthenes comparison. (✅ owner 15 basic section 1)
**Links:** ANC-3007, ANC-3009, ANC-3010.
**Trap:** Ashokan pillars were stone, not brick. Stone working existed before the Mauryas; the empire's scale was new.
**Mains hook:** Use pillars and polish to show how political authority was expressed through material means, while noting that stone working had earlier roots.
**Back:** *"Monolith and mirror-polish: the Mauryan stone idiom."*

### Flashcard ANC-3007 — The Sarnath lion capital and India's national emblem
**Front:** Which Mauryan monument supplies India's national emblem, and what is its date and place?
**Definition (learn):** The lion capital of the Ashokan pillar at Sarnath is the source of India's national emblem. The capital is a Mauryan (Ashokan) work, not Gupta art.
**Breakdown:**
- Ashokan pillar at Sarnath; capital shows four lions (⚠️ standard knowledge, not in owner)
- Displayed in the Sarnath Museum near the excavation site; the column remains at its original location
- Date: c. 250 BCE (Wikipedia lead; owner says 3rd century BCE)
- Adoption: cabinet accepted the state emblem on 29 December 1947 (Wikipedia)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 250 BCE (✅ Wikipedia: Lion Capital of Ashoka). Cabinet accepted state emblem 29 December 1947 (✅ Wikipedia: Lion Capital of Ashoka). Owner lists 3rd century BCE (✅ owner 15).
**Links:** ANC-3006, ANC-3009. Art owner for Mauryan style: Indian Art and Culture (outside this file).
**Trap:** "Lion capital is Gupta art" is wrong. Keep it as Mauryan/Ashokan. Do not confuse the emblem's adoption with the capital's date.
**Mains hook:** Use the capital to show how a Mauryan royal symbol became a modern state symbol; note the polity and culture link.
**Back:** *"Sarnath lions are Ashoka's, the emblem is the Republic's."*

### Flashcard ANC-3008 — Mauryan taxation: prescriptive versus practice
**Front:** Who was taxed under the Mauryas, and why is the Arthashastra's tax list a limited guide to actual collection?
**Definition (learn):** Mauryan taxation covered peasants, artisans and traders. The Arthashastra names many taxes and regulatory offices, but its layered prescriptive model cannot be treated as a complete record of uniform collection.
**Breakdown:**
- Taxed groups: peasants, artisans, traders (✅ owner)
- Arthashastra: prescriptive model, many taxes and offices
- Assessment, collection and storage needed strong machinery
- State regulation was strongest near strategic resources, routes and centres
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Taxes on peasants, artisans and traders (✅ owner 15 must-know facts). Arthashastra is prescriptive, not a complete record (⚠️ owner 15 section 1).
**Links:** ANC-3010, ANC-3013, ANC-3015 (fiscal-military strain).
**Trap:** The Arthashastra's monopolies and taxes do not show uniform state control of the whole economy. Do not write a "fully state-controlled Mauryan economy".
**Mains hook:** Use taxation to show fiscal capacity, then qualify with regional variation and prescription versus practice.
**Back:** *"Peasants, artisans, traders paid; the Arthashastra wrote the rules, not the ledger."*

### Flashcard ANC-3009 — Imperial communication: Ashokan edicts and scripts
**Front:** Which scripts and languages did Ashoka's edicts use, and what did they make visible?
**Definition (learn):** Ashoka's rock and pillar edicts carried royal ethical and political speech across regions. Prakrit in Brahmi predominated, Kharosthi served the north-west, and Greek and Aramaic addressed selected frontier audiences.
**Breakdown:**
- Prakrit in Brahmi: the main script and language
- Kharosthi: north-west
- Greek and Aramaic: selected frontier audiences
